## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `fe3ce145d350b4ab7d1af80a42f80f9b561c4caee636f131fd984fd15e13ee92`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-detect-uncited-sources-even-when-the-text-isnt-copied-word-for-word` (Similarity: 0.8871)

```text
The key difference between uncited writing and plagiarism lies in intent, scope, and process: uncited writing typically involves mechanical formatting errors or forgotten citations in an otherwise student-authored draft, whereas plagiarism involves the deliberate misrepresentation of external text or ideas as one's own original work.

In high school and collegiate classrooms, treating every missing parenthetical citation as malicious academic misconduct damages student trust and ignores the reality of developing research skills. At the same time, excusing blatant copy-pasting as a "simple citation mistake" erodes academic standards. To respond appropriately, educators need clear Uncited / Source Evidence that distinguishes poor citation mechanics from intentional deception.

Below is an educator's guide to distinguishing uncited writing from plagiarism using textual and process evidence.

Checkmark Plagiarism powers source analysis by pairing plagiarism detection with essay writing playback , AI detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Core Distinctions: Uncited Writing vs. Plagiarism
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `bcd3b08187a28b988f2dcef9292ddf0af703f5d6535646fdbd98bc9f1a3332d9`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/6/student-plagiarism-explained-causes-detection-prevention-and-consequences` (Similarity: 0.8380)

```text
1. Process Evidence & Keystrokes

Uncited Writing: Document shows hours of authentic human typing and revisions, but lacks parentheticals. Plagiarism: Document shows an instant wholesale clipboard paste with zero typing.

2. Textual Verbatim Overlap

Uncited Writing: Student summarized factual data in their own words without an in-text tag. Plagiarism: Multiple sentences copied word-for-word without quotation marks.

3. Bibliography & Reference List

Uncited Writing: The source is listed in the Works Cited page, but missing an in-text marker. Plagiarism: The copied source is completely hidden from the bibliography.

4. Response to Teacher Feedback

Uncited Writing: Student immediately points to the source and adds the citation. Plagiarism: Student cannot explain where the text came from or define its vocabulary.
```

### Section: What Checkmark Uncited Evidence Cards Reveal
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `e3a731d82a672c0b9f6a66a1c19a99bed19c76f15182de348a14fcaf987969ab`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-side-by-side-source-quotations-protect-students-from-unfair-similarity-accusations` (Similarity: 0.8913)

```text
When Checkmark analyzes an essay, it separates matches into two distinct categories:

Citation Mechanical Errors (Uncited References): Flags factual data or paraphrased summaries where the student cited the author in the bibliography but forgot in-text parentheticals, recommending formative citation coaching. 
 Direct Plagiarism (Uncredited Matches): Highlights unquoted verbatim blocks copied from web sources, journals, or peer papers with direct links and paste timestamps.

Read more in how Checkmark writing process analysis works .
```

### Section: Comparison: Uncited Writing vs. Intentional Plagiarism
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `cb07626adca8fa825f33a1c98c6c3c4c7f6fe560792020f417e5df16a0819f23`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-detect-uncited-sources-even-when-the-text-isnt-copied-word-for-word` (Similarity: 0.8437)

```text
Uncited Writing (Skill Deficit / Mechanical Error)

Document shows 3+ hours of active human typing. 
 Student rephrased the concept in their own voice. 
 Source may be listed in bibliography without in-text tag. 
 Student fluently explains the research during check-in. 
 Action: Formative feedback & required revision.

Intentional Plagiarism (Misconduct)

Document shows instant wholesale paste ( 
 Verbatim strings copied without quotation marks. 
 Source completely omitted from bibliography. 
 Student unable to explain concepts or vocabulary. 
 Action: Academic integrity referral & penalty.
```

### Section: A 5-Step Educator Decision Tree
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `8c4329dd9823f2b47be513ef7a29714f21a81f7853105c980a035f07077a3085`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/who-should-make-the-final-decision-about-suspected-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.8342)

```text
Educator Citation Audit Decision Tree:

1. Open the Checkmark report to review matched text and writing playback history. 
 2. If the document shows hours of typing and the source is in the bibliography → treat as Uncited Writing. 
 3. If the document shows an instant paste and unquoted verbatim text → investigate as Plagiarism. 
 4. Hold a brief 2-minute conference to assess student comprehension and intent. 
 5. Apply formative revision for mechanical errors or formal integrity protocols for deception.
```
